=
1
claude --permission-mode bypassPermissions
With this variable set, you lose a safety prompt for destructive deletes: a command such as
rm -rf ~
can run without asking you. Set it only in an
isolated environment
whose contents you can afford to lose.
Removals run with no prompt only in
bypassPermissions
mode itself, not in plan mode, and only for what the critical-path check flags in the Bash and
Monitor
tools. That includes commands Claude Code can’t fully parse, which also run with no prompt.
​
Startup notices about the critical-path removal prompt
To check whether
CLAUDE_CODE_EVEN_MORE_DANGEROUSLY_SKIP_PERMISSIONS
is working, read the line Claude Code prints at startup. In a
-p
run, it’s on stderr. In an Agent SDK app, pass the
stderr
option
to read it. The line says one of these:
is set, so dangerous rm commands can run without a prompt
: removals can run with no prompt once managed settings and plugin hooks have loaded. If removals keep getting a prompt or denial, see
Why the critical-path removal prompt stays on
has no effect in this session
: removals keep their usual prompt or denial for the whole session. To change that, find your case under
Why the critical-path removal prompt stays on
has no effect while the Windows registry cannot be read for managed settings
: removals keep their usual prompt or denial, and the variable takes effect with no restart if a later read of the registry succeeds. If removals keep getting a prompt or denial, see
Why the critical-path removal prompt stays on
A missing line doesn’t mean the prompt is on: an Agent SDK app with no
stderr
option sees no line, and removals can still run with no prompt. Otherwise, check that you set the variable in the launch environment, that the session
can enter
bypassPermissions
mode
, and that
claude
isn’t inside another session. Some sessions
start without the variable
.
If you see one of these lines and didn’t set the variable, find where it’s set, such as your shell profile, and remove it.
​
Why the critical-path removal prompt stays on
If
CLAUDE_CODE_EVEN_MORE_DANGEROUSLY_SKIP_PERMISSIONS
has no effect and removals keep their
usual prompt or denial
, check for causes such as these:
Something else handles permission prompts
: a
--permission-prompt-tool
or an Agent SDK
canUseTool
callback
without
--permission-prompts none
, a
PermissionRequest
hook
with any matcher, or a
mod
that handles that event. Remove the hook, mod, tool, or callback and restart
claude
The
VS Code extension
’s chat panel or the
Desktop app
runs the session
: start
claude
in a terminal instead, such as an IDE’s integrated terminal
A rule covers Bash, Edit, or Read
: a
deny or ask rule
that targets one of these tools, at launch or once Claude Code reads it during the session, even if you remove it later. Remove the rule and restart
claude
. To leave a tool out, use
--tools
instead of a deny rule
permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories
is on
: remove the setting from every settings file that sets it to
true
, then restart
claude
claude
runs inside another session
: a
claude
started from a session’s Bash tool or a hook, even with the variable on its command line. Start
claude
from your own shell. Don’t rely on this nested-session limit as a security boundary
A
PreToolUse
hook answers
ask
for a removal
: change what the hook returns
Claude Code starts the session for you
:
background sessions
,
agent team
teammates in split panes, and sessions a
Remote Control
server creates start without the variable. Start
claude
from your own shell
Managed settings can’t be read
: Claude Code hasn’t successfully read your
managed settings
sources. If server-managed settings keep failing to load, see
Remote managed settings failed to load
. For any other failure that isn’t a registry query, ask whoever manages that source to fix it
The Windows registry can’t be queried
: on Windows and in WSL, the variable needs an answer from the registry, even when
WSL doesn’t use it for settings
. The query can fail all session on a non-English Windows, in WSL with interop off, or in a container on a WSL2 kernel. If interop is off, turn it on. Otherwise, run those removals yourself
​
Remove-Item in PowerShell
When you enable the
PowerShell tool
, Claude Code gives
Remove-Item
and the
cmd
built-ins
rd
,
rmdir
,
del
, and
erase
their own checks, separate from the
rm
critical-path list. For
Remove-Item
, the outcome depends on the target, and the first matching case applies:
System paths
: the filesystem root and its top-level directories, drive roots and their top-level directories, and your home directory. Claude Code denies the command in every mode, without asking you.
Wildcards
: a bare
*
, or any target ending in
/*
or
\*
, including a glob under a shell variable such as
$dir/*
. Claude Code denies the command in every mode, without asking you, before the
classifier
sees it.
Your working directory or one of its parents, with
-Recurse
: Claude Code treats the command like any other that needs approval in your permission mode, so it asks you in modes that ask, sends it to the classifier in
auto
mode, and denies it in
dontAsk
mode.
bypassPermissions
mode skips this check.
The system-paths case also applies to
rd
,
rmdir
,
del
, and
erase
when Claude runs them through
cmd
, as in
cmd /c rd /s /q C:\Users
. By default, Claude Code denies such a command in every mode, without asking you. This
cmd
check requires Claude Code v2.1.283 or later.
When judging a
cmd
target, Claude Code treats a PowerShell variable that follows literal text as empty. That makes
cmd /c rd /s /q "C:\$name"
a removal of
C:\
, so it is denied too. A trailing wildcard counts as the folder it empties, so
cmd /c del /q C:\*
is denied and
cmd /c del /q dist\*
in your project is not.
To turn the
cmd
check off, set
CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_POWERSHELL_CMD_RM_DENY=1
in the environment that launches Claude Code. Claude Code ignores this variable in a settings file’s
env
block.
Remove-Item
on a system path stays denied either way.
​
See also
Permissions
: allow, ask, and deny rules; managed policies
Configure auto mode
: tell the classifier which infrastructure your organization trusts
Hooks
: custom permission logic via
PreToolUse
and
PermissionRequest
hooks
Security
: safeguards and best practices
Sandboxing
: filesystem and network isolation for Bash commands
Non-interactive mode
: run Claude Code with the
-p
flag

The most common causes are an LLM gateway or proxy that drops the request for review or the results, and a platform, region, or credential that doesn’t have server-side checks yet. Claude Code falls back to its own classifier requests. On any connection, this happens when the response ends before the review results arrive or the results arrive in a form Claude Code can’t read. An LLM gateway or proxy that cuts responses short or rewrites the results can cause either. On a direct connection to the Anthropic API, it also happens when the server’s check fails for the action, for example by timing out. `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/errors#the-server-returned-no-safety-verdict`,children:`The server returned no safety verdict`}),` covers the denial message, what happens when denials repeat, and what to do.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To skip asking the server and always use Claude Code’s own classifier requests, set `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/env-vars`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_SERVER=0`})}),`. Claude Code checks these commands before they run, and no `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/permissions#manage-permissions`,children:`allow rule`}),` approves one whose target it recognizes as a critical path. Claude Code finds a critical-path removal whether it sits inside the nested form, as in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`(rm -rf ~)`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`echo \"$(rm -rf ~)\"`}),`, or elsewhere in the same command.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Inline scripts`}),`: a script passed to `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`sh`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`bash`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`zsh`}),`, or a similar POSIX shell with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`-c`}),`, as in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`bash -c 'rm -rf ~'`}),`.`,`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`When the script is double-quoted, the invoking shell expands its variables before the inner shell receives the script. In `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`find . -name '*.tmp' -exec sh -c \"rm -rf \\\\\"$1\\\\\"/*\" _ {} \\\\;`}),`, the command expands to a removal from the filesystem root once per match, and Claude Code treats it as a critical-path removal.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`A single-quoted script that binds `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`$1`}),` to a real value, as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`sh -c 'rm -rf \"$1\"/*' _ {}`}),` does, isn’t flagged.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To turn off the check on a critical path typed directly in a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`-c`}),` script, such as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`~`}),`, set `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/env-vars#variables`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_INLINE_SHELL_RM_PROMPT=1`})}),` in the environment that launches Claude Code.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`rewrite-a-flagged-command`,children:`Rewrite a flagged command`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`How to rewrite a command so it passes the check depends on which of the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#other-targets-that-count-as-critical-paths`,children:`other targets`}),` it uses:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsxs(_components.strong,{children:[`A glob or trailing slash under a variable such as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`$DIR`})]}),`: guard each expansion so the shell stops with an error when the variable is unset or empty, as in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:'rm -rf \"${DIR:?}\"/*'}),`, or use a literal path.
